**cf. Judgment.** The useful syllogism = the coupling of propositions = Syllogismos <note>original: "suluhmimûs"; the Arabic transliteration of the Greek term for syllogism</note> **Inference**

**Conclusion** (Result, results); **necessary**; **possible**

**Rule:** Demonstrative syllogism <note>original: "qiyas burhani"</note> **demonstrative conclusion**; premise **(Major Premise)**

That they should not use a demonstrative syllogism consisting of two negative premises, nor two universal ones, nor two particular ones at all, nor two indefinite ones, nor a particular and a specific one whatsoever. <note>These are standard Aristotelian rules for valid logical deductions.</note>

**To include/imply**

Indeed, the Creator is the cause of existing things, the reason for all beings, their Originator (**Creator**), Inventor, Bringer into existence, Sustainer, Completer, Perfecter, and the one who brings them to their best states and furthest goals; He is Everlasting, and nothing of annihilation ever occurs to Him.

Particular (**partial**); Universal (**entire**); The quantifier of statements (**Boundaries of expression**) <note>original: "sur al-aqawil"; 'sur' literally means a fence or wall, used in logic to denote universal or particular quantifiers like 'all' or 'some'.</note>

Indeed, the quantifiers only restrict the attributes to the subject.

**Animal Soul.** The vegetative, appetitive soul (**Vegetative Soul**); souls, **Soul**

The human, rational soul (**Human Soul**); the animal, irascible soul <note>The "irascible" soul is the seat of anger and courage in classical psychology.</note>

The angelic, law-giving soul (**Angelic Soul**); the intellectual soul of the wise (**Soul of Wisdom**)

**Partial/Particular.** The particular human soul. **D.** The soul is a simple, spiritual substance, intelligible, celestial, luminous, all-knowing, perceptive, and active. ۞ The state of the particular soul before its connection to bodies ۞ its state after its separation from bodies ۞

The Universal Celestial Soul is the soul of the entire world ۞ (**Universal Soul**)

The Universal Celestial Soul is knowing in actuality, while the particular souls are knowing in potentiality. <note>In this context, the Universal Soul contains all knowledge ready-to-use, while individual human souls must learn to unlock it.</note>

Thus, every particular soul that possesses more knowledge and more refined handiwork is closer to the Universal Soul, due to its proximity in relation and its strong resemblance to it ۞ 28.

**cf. Universal Soul, see: World.** **The action of the soul:** Indeed, the soul is a spiritual substance by its very nature; when it interacts with any body, it makes it alive like itself. The soul has two powers: one is the cognitive and the other is the active. Through its cognitive power, it extracts the imprints of known things from their **matter** <term>Hyle: the fundamental, formless "stuff" of the universe</term> and represents them within itself, so that the essence of its substance becomes like matter for those imprints, while they exist within it as forms; [it acts] through its active power... <note>The text continues on the next page regarding the active power.</note>

<summary>This page defines logical terms regarding the syllogism and quantifiers before transitioning into a Neoplatonic treatise on the Soul (Nafs). It describes the hierarchy of souls—vegetative, animal, rational, and angelic—and the relationship between the Universal Soul and individual human souls.</summary>

**Judgment** <note>original German/Greek: *Urtheil*, *apophansis*, *protasis*</note> **proposition** <note>original Arabic: *qadiyya*</note> **closed** [definite] ruling.
Speech consists of two types: at times, truth and falsehood are apparent in it — **also** called [simple] speech — **not bounded** [unbounded] **bounded** [definite] — both are evident, and at other times they are hidden.
**bejahend** [affirmative] <note>original: *mujib*</note> **specific** <note>original: *makhsus*</note> **neglected** [indefinite] **vague** [unbounded].

Contradictory <note>original: *mutanaqidan*</note> **they contradict** in quantity **in quantity** different in quality.
Conversion of the proposition **Conversion of the judgment** contraries **they are opposed.**
**Connection of 2 judgments** [syllogism] **Parts of the judgment: Antecedent.**
**Middle term** two limits **the 2 terms of the judgment** result **Consequent** premise **etc.** The first figure **I. Figure** sharing in one limit **i. e. term.**
If an attribute is judged upon an object, that is called a **binary proposition etc.** If one of the three times [tenses] is joined to the proposition, it is called a **ternary proposition**, and if one of the three elements—which are the possible, the necessary, and the impossible—is added to one of the ternary propositions, it is called a **quaternary proposition**. <note>A quaternary proposition includes Subject, Predicate, Time, and Modality.</note>

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical glossary of philosophical and theological terms, moving from the logic and propositions of the previous page into psychology (the nature of the Intellect), physics (qualities like heat and cold), and cosmology (the nature of the world and the World-Soul). It primarily covers terms starting with "V" and "W".</meta>

**Reason** <note>Arabic: *ʿaql*</note> cf. **Soul** <note>Arabic: *nafs*</note> **Nature** <note>Arabic: *tabīʿa*</note> **Object of reason** <note>Arabic: *maʿqūl*</note>

**The human intellect** — it is nothing other than human souls which have become knowledgeable in actuality after having been knowledgeable in potentiality; they only become knowledgeable in actuality after the forms of the identities of things are obtained within them through the way of the senses, and the forms of their essences [quiddities] through the way of thought and reflection. ۞ **The active intellect** — which is a simple substance that perceives the realities of things; from this intellect, things emanated upon the Universal Celestial Soul. ۞ **Spiritual intellectual powers**. **Innate reason** <note>Arabic: *al-ʿaql al-gharīzī*</note>. **Rational speech** <note>Arabic: *nuṭq*</note>.

**Difference** in **quality**, in **quantity**.
**To perfect**, **to complete**.
**Premise** <note>Arabic: *muqaddima*</note>: **particular**, **universal**, **productive premise** <note>A logical premise that leads to a valid conclusion</note>.
**Existence** [the finding of a thing]; **being**. **The existing** — **Non-existence**.
**Virtue** [excellence].

**Waking** and **Sleep**: Waking is nothing other than the soul's use of the five senses, and sleep is nothing other than its cessation of their use.
**Growing** <note>referring to organic or plant life</note>. Opposite: **Inanimate** [solid matter]. **Heat**. **Cold**.
**Scale** [balance]. **Scale of weights**: the balance by which weights are known. **Logical scale** <note>referring to the rules of logic used to "weigh" arguments</note>. **Weighed**.
**Truth** [veracity]. **Lie** [falsehood]. **To endure** [duration].

**When** <note>as a category of time; Greek: *pote*</note>: it is the combination of a substance with time.
The **Quiddity** <note>the "What-ness" or essence of a thing; Arabic: *māhiyya*</note> (**Identity**) = **Essence**.
**Melody** [manner of singing]: a composition made of proportional tones.
**Change of the winds**: the redirection of the four winds and their shifts.
**Way** [path]. **Expanse** [space].
**World** <note>Arabic: *ʿālam*</note>. **The physical world**: the world of bodies, which are of two types: celestial and natural. **This world**: the world of **generation and corruption** <note>The sublunary realm where things are born and decay</note>. **The other world** [the hereafter]: This world and the hereafter are two opposing abodes; their names are correlative, but their meanings, realities, and attributes are different and contrary. ۞
**World-Soul**: The soul of the world is a single soul, just as its body is a single body.

<meta>This page continues the philosophical and theological glossary from the previous page, specifically focusing on terms starting with "W" in German (corresponding to terms for knowledge, will, and the world soul) and the categorization of various branches of science. The text transitions from cosmology into a formal classification of knowledge, likely influenced by the Brethren of Purity (Ikhwan al-Safa).</meta>

With all its spheres, planets, elements, and offspring ۞ the **Soul of the World** <note>Arabic: *nafs al-ʿālam*</note> had **universal actions** through universal powers, **generic actions** through generic powers, **specific actions** through specific powers, and **individual actions** through individual powers. These powers were named, according to their actions, generic, specific, and individual souls. The souls multiplied according to their various different powers, and their powers multiplied according to their diverse actions, just as the body of the celestial sphere multiplied according to the difference of its shapes, and its shapes multiplied according to the difference of its accidental properties ۞

**Practical Crafts** <note>Arabic: *al-ṣanāʾiʿ al-ʿamaliyya*; German: *pract. الصنائع العملية*</note>. **Theoretical Crafts** <note>Arabic: *al-ṣanāʾiʿ al-ʿilmiyya*; German: *theor. الصنائع العلمية*</note>. **Work/Craft** <note>German: *Werk*</note>.
**Equipping/Preparation** <note>German: *Zurüstung*</note>. **Tool/Instrument** <note>German: *Werkzeug*</note>.
**Rational Being** <note>German: *Wesen mit Vern[unft]*</note>. **Entities/Realities** <note>German: *die W[esen]*</note>. **Essence/Nature** <note>German: *Wesen*</note>.
**A possessor of knowledge and discernment**.
**Contradiction** <note>German: *Widerspruch*</note>. **Submission/Dedication** <note>German: *widmen*</note>.
**Quality** <note>Arabic: *al-kayf*; German: *d[as] Wie*</note> consists of two types: **physical** and **spiritual**. The physical is what <note>original: *ποιόν* (poion)</note> is perceived by the senses; the spiritual is what is perceived by the mind.
**Quantity** <note>Arabic: *al-kammiyya*; German: *d[as] Wieviel*</note> is divided into two types: **continuous** and **discrete** <note>original: *ποσόν* (poson)</note>.
**Choice/Free Will** <note>Arabic: *ikhtiyār*; German: *fr[eier] Wille*</note>.
**Cosmic/Existential measure** <note>German: *W[elt] maass*</note> — **Angle** <note>German: *Winkel*</note>: **obtuse**, **right**, **acute**.
**Actions/Effects** <note>German: *Wirken*</note>. **Influence** <note>German: *Einwirken*</note>. **Acting/Effective** <note>German: *Wirkend*</note>.
**Knowledge** <note>German: *Wissen*</note>. **Known object** <note>German: *Gewusst = Wissensobj[ekt]*</note>. **Knowledge** <note>German: *Wissen*</note>:
It only exists after teaching and learning ۞ Knowledge is a state within the soul of the knower ۞ Knowledge is simply the image of the thing known in the soul of the knower ۞ The foundations of known things are taken by way of the senses ۞ **Science/Knowledge** <note>German: *Wissenschaft*</note>.

->**Classification of the Sciences**<-

I. **Preliminary Studies/Literature** <note>Arabic: *ʿilm al-adab*; German: *Vorstudien*</note>:
a. **Reading**; b. **Writing** and **Grammar**; c. **Social Conduct**; d. **Poetry** and **Prosody**; e. **Oracles and Divination**; f. **The Science of Magic**; g. **The Science of Letters and Trades**; h. **The Science of Buying, Selling, Commerce, Farming, and Breeding**; i. **The Science of Biographies and History**.

II. **Religious/Legal Sciences** <note>Arabic: *al-ʿulūm al-sharʿiyya*; German: *Religionsges[etzliche] W[issenschaft]*</note>:
a. **The Science of Revelation**; b. **The Science of Interpretation**; c. **The Science of Jurisprudence**, involving traditions and legal rulings; d. **The Science of Narrations and Reports**; e. **The Science of Remembrance, Exhortation, Asceticism, Sufism, and the Science of Dream Interpretation**.

<summary>This page defines the hierarchical powers of the Universal Soul and provides a lexicon of philosophical terms ranging from "Quality" and "Quantity" to "Knowledge." It concludes with a structured classification of the sciences, divided into secular preliminary studies (like grammar and trade) and religious sciences (like jurisprudence and mysticism).</summary>
<keywords>World Soul, Classification of Sciences, Quality (Poion), Quantity (Poson), Knowledge (Wissen), Religious Law (Shari'a), Preliminary Studies (Adab), Sufism</keywords>
